3144 AE5E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3145 AE60 0450  20 	b *r0
3146               	;
3147               	; 	' THE FONT'S COLOUR TABLE WAS 472 BYTES OF ONE REPEATED VALUE, which is a
3148               	; 	' fifth of what the fixed area had left. DEFINE COLOR copies such a table
3149               	; 	' out of ROM into all three screen thirds; writing the constant straight
3150               	; 	' into the colour table does the same job for the price of a loop, and
3151               	; 	' the loop runs once, at boot.
3152               	; 	'
3153               	; 	' The colour table is at >2000 and the VDP mirrors it once per screen
3154               	; 	' third, >800 apart. Both numbers were read out of the GENERATED assembly
3155               	; 	' -- define_color's `ai r0,>2000` and LDIRVM3's `ai r0,>0800` -- rather
3156               	; 	' than assumed, because a wrong base here would paint over the pattern
3157               	; 	' table and the failure would look like corrupt artwork.
3158               	; 	'
3159               	; 	' Paced: a few hundred VDP writes in one frame are silently dropped.
3160               	; 	' THE SCANNER'S COLOURS ARE THREE BLOCKS REPEATED SIXTEEN TIMES EACH, so
3161               	; 	' the table shipped 384 bytes to say 24 bytes' worth. Same trick as the
3162               	; 	' font: write the colour table directly. >2000 is the colour table's base
3163               	; 	' and >800 the stride between screen thirds -- the same layout DEFINE COLOR
3164               	; 	' walks for us in setup_font, which this cannot use because it is cheaper to
3165               	; 	' expand 24 bytes here than to ship 384 in the bank.
3166               	; 	' NOTHING IS UNDER THE GROUND FLOOR, SO NOTHING SHOULD BE GREEN THERE.
3167               	; 	' A floor bar is five pixels of yellow over three of the green air
3168               	; 	' belonging to the floor BELOW it (see SLAB), which is right for three of
3169               	; 	' the four bars and wrong for the last one: below floor 0 there is no
3170               	; 	' floor, only the scanner, and the green read as a strip of shop with
3171               	; 	' nothing in it.
3172               	; 	'
3173               	; 	' NO NEW CHARACTER IS NEEDED, BECAUSE THE COLOUR TABLE HAS THREE COPIES
3174               	; 	' -- one per eight screen rows -- and the bands land such that each bar
3175               	; 	' sits in a different third:
3176               	; 	'
3177               	; 	'     band 0 roof   rows  1-5    bar row  5   third 0
3178               	; 	'     band 1        rows  6-10   bar row 10   third 1
3179               	; 	'     band 2        rows 11-15   bar row 15   third 1
3180               	; 	'     band 3 GROUND rows 16-20   bar row 20   third 2
3181               	; 	'
3182               	; 	' so recolouring SLAB in third 2 alone reaches the ground floor's bar and
3183               	; 	' nothing else. The same third also holds the scanner, but the scanner
3184               	; 	' does not use these characters. This is the same mechanism that once hid
3185               	; 	' a bug for months (CLAUDE.md 3A, the blanked-in-one-third note) used
3186               	; 	' deliberately for once.
3187               	; 	'
3188               	; 	' Only the three bottom lines change, and only their BACKGROUND: SLAB has
3189               	; 	' no ink there and SLABE's brick keeps its grey.
3190               	; floor0_colour:
3191               cvb_FLOOR0_COLOUR
3192               	; 	' TWO CHARACTERS, THE SAME THREE SCAN LINES, ONE ROUTINE. Both blocks
3193               	; 	' were written out in full and differed only in which character and which
3194               	; 	' colour byte -- eleven statements twice, including the multiply-by-eight
3195               	; 	' done by doubling (`*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, which clobbers r0,
3196               	; 	' CLAUDE.md 3A).
3197               	; 	f0c = CH_SLAB
3198 AE62 0200  20 	li r0,24576   ; 96*256
     AE64 6000     
3199 AE66 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AE68 20D0     
3200               	; 	f0v = 177			' LYELL on BLACK -- no ink, so the
3201 AE6A 0200  20 	li r0,45312   ; 177*256
     AE6C B100     
3202 AE6E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AE70 20D8     
3203               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows			' background is all that shows
3204 AE72 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AE74 A980     
3205 AE76 AE92     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3206               	; 	f0c = CH_SLABE
3207 AE78 0200  20 	li r0,24832   ; 97*256
     AE7A 6100     
3208 AE7C D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AE7E 20D0     
3209               	; 	f0v = 225			' GRAY brick on BLACK
3210 AE80 0200  20 	li r0,57600   ; 225*256
     AE82 E100     
3211 AE84 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AE86 20D8     
3212               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows
3213 AE88 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AE8A A980     
3214 AE8C AE92     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3215               	; 	RETURN
3216 AE8E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3217 AE90 0450  20 	b *r0
3218               	;
3219               	; 	' The last three scan lines of character f0c, in the BOTTOM SCREEN THIRD
3220               	; 	' only -- which is why this cannot be a DEFINE COLOR: that always writes
3221               	; 	' all three thirds (define_color takes the LDIRVM3 path unconditionally).
3222               	; f0_rows:
3223               cvb_F0_ROWS
3224               	; 	#f0a = 12288			' colour table, bottom screen third
3225 AE92 0200  20 	li r0,12288
     AE94 3000     
3226 AE96 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__F0A
     AE98 2134     
3227               	; 	#f0b = f0c
3228 AE9A D020  34 	movb @cvb_F0C,r0
     AE9C 20D0     
3229 AE9E 0980  32 	srl r0,8
3230 AEA0 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__F0B
     AEA2 2138     
3231               	; 	#f0b = #f0b + #f0b
3232 AEA4 A020  34 	a @cvb__F0B,r0
     AEA6 2138     
3233 AEA8 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__F0B
     AEAA 2138     
3234               	; 	#f0b = #f0b + #f0b
3235 AEAC A020  34 	a @cvb__F0B,r0
     AEAE 2138

3598               	;
3599               	; 	' ------------------------------------------- ALPHA LOCK, CALIBRATED
3600               	; 	' On the TI, ALPHA LOCK shares a line with the joystick's VERTICAL axis.
3601               	; 	' Latched down it reports a direction that is NEVER RELEASED. Every other
3602               	; 	' game in this repo dodges this by not reading up/down at all; this one
3603               	; 	' cannot, because down is the duck and up is the elevator.
3604               	; 	'
3605               	; 	' THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS REFUSED TO START UNTIL THE AXIS CLEARED, AND
3606               	; 	' THAT WAS THE WRONG CALL. Classic99 defaults to invertcaps=1, so the TI
3607               	; 	' sees ALPHA LOCK DOWN when the host's Caps Lock is UP -- the normal
3608               	; 	' state. The game then sat on its title screen for ever waiting for a
3609               	; 	' condition the player had no reason to suspect, which presents as "the
3610               	; 	' title comes up and it will not start". A check that turns a survivable
3611               	; 	' input quirk into a dead game is worse than no check.
3612               	; 	'
3613               	; 	' So: sample the axis for 40 frames BEFORE any input can reasonably have
3614               	; 	' been given. A direction held for essentially all of them is not a player
3615               	; 	' -- it is the key. Record it, say so, and then IGNORE that direction for
3616               	; 	' the whole game, which makes ALPHA LOCK harmless instead of fatal.
3617               	; alock_cal:
3618               cvb_ALOCK_CAL
3619               	; 	alku = 0

     B286 2175

3830               	; 	' FIRE ONLY, AND THAT IS A DECISION RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.
3831               	; 	'
3832               	; 	' `1` used to start the game as well, because on the TI joystick fire is
3833               	; 	' TAB -- neither guessable nor forgiving, since Windows treats a stray TAB
3834               	; 	' as a focus change and moves the whole window away. CLAUDE.md 3A still
3835               	; 	' says not to make FIRE the only way out of a title screen, and this screen
3836               	; 	' is the named exception.
3837               	; 	'
3838               	; 	' WHAT IT BUYS IS THE 838 SEQUENCE. Every digit on this screen now means
3839               	; 	' exactly one thing: a step of the cheat code, or a reset of it. While `1`
3840               	; 	' also meant START, mistyping one digit of 8-3-8 did not merely reset the
3841               	; 	' sequence -- it began a game, which is a far worse outcome than having to
3842               	; 	' type the code again, and it is the single most likely typo since 1 is
3843               	; 	' next to nothing on the sequence but adjacent to everything on a keypad.
3844               	; 	'
3845               	; 	' The hazard the digit guarded against is real but recoverable: a TAB that
3846               	; 	' moves focus leaves the title on screen and the player clicks back. A
3847               	; 	' game started by accident cannot be undone at all.

3865               	; 	' wanted next. That worked -- but only because 8-3-8 ALTERNATES, so holding
3866               	; 	' 8 cannot advance past the first step. It is an accident of the sequence
3867               	; 	' rather than a design, and it had two costs: 8,5,3,9,8 opened this page as
3868               	; 	' readily as 8,3,8, and a key that READS as held for many frames got a free
3869               	; 	' walk through the state machine.
3870               	; 	'
3871               	; 	' That second one is not hypothetical on this machine. ALPHA LOCK shorts a
3872               	; 	' keyboard line -- and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps, so it reads DOWN
3873               	; 	' with the host's Caps Lock UP -- and this page was reached twice from
3874               	; 	' single keypresses while testing an unrelated change.
3875               	; 	'
3876               	; 	' The reset is the DEFAULT rather than a test for a particular wrong digit:
3877               	; 	' Bust-A-Bobble resets only on a stray 3 and still lets 8,5,3,8 through.

4245 B72C 04C0  14 	clr r0
4246 B72E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_KANIM
     B730 2101     
4247               	;
4248               	; 	' HARRY STARTS ON FLOOR 2 AT THE WEST EDGE OF SCREEN 7, and that is as
4249               	; 	' far left as the clock allows. The research says "the second-floor
4250               	; 	' elevator door", which is screen 3, and it cannot be done:
4251               	; 	'
4252               	; 	'   spawn      escape     round
4253               	; 	'   screen 3   119.9 s    100 s   -- impossible
4254               	; 	'   screen 5   108.1 s    100 s   -- impossible
4255               	; 	'   screen 6   102.3 s    100 s   -- impossible
4256               	; 	'   screen 7    96.4 s    100 s   -- 3.6 s of room
4257               	; 	'
4258               	; 	' HE STARTS AT THE LIFT NOW, AND THAT COST HIM A SPEED CHANGE.
4259               	; 	'
4260               	; 	' His escape route ZIGZAGS -- east along floor 2 to its escalator, WEST
4261               	; 	' along floor 3 to that one, then east again along the roof to the door.
4262               	; 	' From the lift on screen 3 that is 5,120 px against the 4,208 he walked
4263               	; 	' from screen 7, so at his old 1.75 px a pass he needed 105 s of a 100 s
4264               	; 	' round: he could never escape at all, and one of the two loss conditions
4265               	; 	' would quietly have stopped existing. This block used to say exactly that
4266               	; 	' and conclude the lift was impossible. It was impossible AT THAT SPEED.
4267               	; 	'
4268               	; 	' AND QUARTER-PIXELS COULD NOT EXPRESS THE ANSWER. A quarter of a pixel
4269               	; 	' per pass is worth about TEN SECONDS of his route, so the two candidate
4270               	; 	' values straddled the buzzer with nothing in between:
4271               	; 	'
4272               	; 	'   2.00 px/pass   escapes at 102.4 s of a 100 s round   never escapes
4273               	; 	'   2.25 px/pass   escapes at  91.0 s                    nine seconds early
4274               	; 	'
4275               	; 	' Worse, 2.25 was not reachable at all: the accumulator below had two
4276               	; 	' drain steps and therefore a hard ceiling of 2 px a pass, so hsp4 = 9
4277               	; 	' silently delivered 2.00 and he fell two thirds of a screen short of his
4278               	; 	' own escape. See the accumulator comment for the full account.
4279               	; 	'
4280               	; 	' Sixteenths with three drains put the step at about 2.5 s instead of 10,
4281               	; 	' and hsp64 = 35 (2.1875 px/pass) lands him at 96.3 s of the 100 --
4282               	; 	' escaping as the timer expires, which is what was asked for.
4283               	; 	'
4284               	; 	' 35 AND NOT 34, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE PLAYER'S OWN SCREEN. Measured
4285               	; 	' in play at 34: standing on an ordinary screen he escaped with one timer

4471               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4472               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4473               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4474               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.

4478               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4479               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.

4484               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4485               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him

4518 B8B6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4519 B8B8 0450  20 	b *r0
4520               	;
4521               	; 	' ======================================================================
4522               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4523               	; 	' ======================================================================
4524               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4525               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4526               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4527               	; 	' of running.
4528               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4529               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4530               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4531               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4532               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4533               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4534               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4535               	; 	'
4536               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4537               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4538               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4539               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4540               	; 	'
4541               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4542               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4543               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4544               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4545               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4546               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4547               	; 	'
4548               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4549               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4550               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4551               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4552               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4553               	; 	'
4554               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4555               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4556               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4557               	; draw_screen:
4558               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4559               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4560 B8BA 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8BC A980     
4561 B8BE EA7E     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4562               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4563 B8C0 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8C2 A980     
4564 B8C4 BCAE     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND
4565               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4566 B8C6 04C0  14 	clr r0
4567 B8C8 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     B8CA 21B4     
4568               cv125
4569               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4570               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4571               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4572               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4573               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4574               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4575               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4576               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4577               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4578               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.
4579               	; 		WAIT
4580 B8CC 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8CE A980

4667               	;
4668               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4669               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4670               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4671               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4672               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4673               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4674               	; 	'
4675               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4676               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4677               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4678               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4679               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4680               	; 	'
4681               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4682               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4683               	; 	' above it at all.
4684               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4685               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4686               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4687               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4688               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4689               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4690               	; 	'
4691               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4692               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4693               	; beam_one:
4694               cvb_BEAM_ONE

5228               	; 			' AND HOW MANY FLOORS CARRY ONE AT ALL. The gates above
5229               	; 			' decide what KIND a hazard is; this decides whether the
5230               	; 			' floor has one.
5231               	; 			'
5232               	; 			' The kind-arrival rule downgrades anything that has not
5233               	; 			' arrived yet to a beach ball rather than leaving the floor
5234               	; 			' empty, so that a floor is never bare -- and the effect on
5235               	; 			' Krook 1 was a ball on all FOUR floors of every populated
5236               	; 			' screen. All four bands are on screen at once, so that is
5237               	; 			' four identical balls in view at all times, which is not
5238               	; 			' what "merely a few beach balls" describes.
5239               	; 			'
5240               	; 			' Per-band count was never the problem: only one slot is
5241               	; 			' live until Krook 6. It is the number of OCCUPIED FLOORS,
5242               	; 			' and it had no ramp at all.
5243               	; 			'
5244               	; 			' Floors 1 and 3 stay clear on Krook 1, floor 1 joins on
5245               	; 			' Krook 2, and the roof on Krook 3. ALTERNATING rather than
5246               	; 			' clearing the top or bottom half, so the empty floors do
5247               	; 			' not stack into a visibly dead region that reads as a bug
5248               	; 			' -- and the ROOF is last, because that is where the round

5288               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
5289               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
5290               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
5291               	; 			'
5292               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
5293               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
5294               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
5295               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
5296               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
5297               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
5298               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
5299               	; 			'
5300               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
5301               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
5302               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
5303               	; 			'
5304               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
5305               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs
5306               	; 			' past is not what this rule is about.

5334 BE78 D060  34 	movb @cvb_LK,r1

     BE7E 21DE     
5336 BE80 0980  32 	srl r0,8

     BE84 2288     
5338 BE86 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5339               	; 			' A RADIO NEEDS A CELL, NOT A PIXEL, because it is drawn as
5340               	; 			' characters. Divided by repeated subtraction: `/` compiles
5341               	; 			' to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A), and this runs once
5342               	; 			' per screen where the divide would run per obstacle -- so
5343               	; 			' the loop is the cheaper of the two, and the only one that
5344               	; 			' cannot be got wrong by a rounding rule.
5345               	; 			obc(li) = 0
5346 BE88 04C1  14 	clr r1
5347 BE8A D020  34 	movb @cvb_LI,r0
     BE8C 21DE     
5348 BE8E 0980  32 	srl r0,8
5349 BE90 0220  22 	ai r0,array_OBC
     BE92 2270     
5350 BE94 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5351               	; 			' PER KIND, not one global speed (see start_krook).
5352               	; 			' THE SPEED IS A PROPERTY OF THE KIND, and obs() only ever
5353               	; 			' held a copy of it -- set from the kind here, zeroed for a
5354               	; 			' radio below, and read in one place. upd_obst derives it
5355               	; 			' from obk() instead, which is 8 bytes of RAM back and one
5356               	; 			' fewer thing that can disagree with itself.
5357               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5358               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5359               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5360               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5361               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5362               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5363               	; 			'
5364               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5365               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads them out
5366               	; 			' so they arrive in sequence instead of as one wall.
5367               	; 			'
5368               	; 			' THE SECOND ONE IS A FIXED DISTANCE BEHIND THE FIRST, AND
5369               	; 			' THAT DISTANCE IS SET BY THE JUMP.
5370               	; 			'
5371               	; 			' It used to be `(lx AND 63) + 64` -- a masked byte out of
5372               	; 			' the placement table plus a constant -- so the gap between
5373               	; 			' the two hazards was whatever those two bytes happened to
5374               	; 			' differ by. Measured across the five populated screens it
5375               	; 			' came out 46, 46, 70, 70, 70 px. Screens 2 and 4 flank the
5376               	; 			' elevator, so the store had a visibly tighter pair on one
5377               	; 			' side of it than the other, which is exactly how it was
5378               	; 			' reported from play.
5379               	; 			'
5380               	; 			' Both numbers are too small, which is the real fault. Kelly
5381               	; 			' closes on an oncoming hazard at WALKSP + the hazard's own
5382               	; 			' speed = 6 px a pass, and a jump lasts about 30 passes, so
5383               	; 			' a jump eats ~120 px of closing distance. At 46 or 70 there
5384               	; 			' is NO screen where he can land between the two: he clears
5385               	; 			' the first and comes down on the second.
5386               	; 			'
5387               	; 			' HAZGAP is that distance with margin. The first hazard
5388               	; 			' still lands where the table puts it, so the placement
5389               	; 			' variety is unchanged; only the pairing is now a fact about
5390               	; 			' the jump rather than an accident of two bytes.
5391               	; 			' assets/checkspace.py measures it and fails the build.
5392               	; 			' THE SECOND SLOT IS MEASURED FROM THE FIRST, not from its
5393               	; 			' own table byte. Adding HAZGAP to its own `lx AND 63` would
5394               	; 			' still leave the GAP varying by up to 63 px between screens
5395               	; 			' -- the same fault in a politer form, and the reason the two
5396               	; 			' sides of the elevator differed in the first place. Holding
5397               	; 			' slot 0's stagger and adding to that makes the gap exactly
5398               	; 			' HAZGAP on every screen, while slot 0 still lands wherever
5399               	; 			' the table puts it, so the placement variety is untouched.
5400               	; 			stag = lx AND 63
5401 BE96 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0

5480 BF54 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5481               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5482               	; 			'
5483               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5484               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5485               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5486               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5487               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5488               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5489               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5490               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5491               	; 			'
5492               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5493               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5494               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5495               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5496               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5497               	; 			'
5498               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5499               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5500               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5501               	; 			'
5502               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5503               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5504               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5505               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5506               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5507               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5508               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5509 BF56 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BF58 21ED

6397               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6398               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6399               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6400               	; 		'
6401               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6402               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6403               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6404               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6405               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6406               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6407               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6408               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6409               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6410               	; 		'
6411               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6412               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6413               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6414               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6415               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6416               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

6718               	;
6719               	; 	' --- running
6720               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6721               	; 	'
6722               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6723               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6724               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6725               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6726               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6727               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6728               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6729               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6730               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6731               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.

6941               	; 		' AN ODOMETER, NOT A CLOCK -- pixels travelled, exactly like
6942               	; 		' Harry's hanim. It counted PASSES once, which made his legs cycle
6943               	; 		' slower on the escalator screens while he still ran at the same
6944               	; 		' speed; that was fixed by counting the frame DELTA instead, which
6945               	; 		' traded one mismatch for another -- his legs then kept wall-clock
6946               	; 		' time while his body still moved once per pass, so his stride
6947               	; 		' slipped against the ground whenever the loop was busy.
6948               	; 		'
6949               	; 		' Distance settles it. A pose per 8 px at any frame rate, and a

7061               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
7062               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
7063               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
7064               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
7065               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
7066               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
7067               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
7068               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
7069               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
7070               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
7071               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
7072               	; 	'
7073               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
7074               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
7075               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
7076               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at

7636               	;
7637               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7638               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7639               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7640               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7641               	; 	'
7642               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7643               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7644               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7645               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7646               	; 	'
7647               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7648               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7649               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7650               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7651               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7652               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7653               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7654               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7655               	; 	'
7656               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7657               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7658               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7659               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7660               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7661               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7662               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7663               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7664               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7665               	; 	'
7666               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7667               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7668               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7669               	; wipe_shelf:
7670               cvb_WIPE_SHELF

7744               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7745               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7746               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7747               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.

7751               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above

7753               	; 	' nothing.
7754               	; 	'
7755               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7756               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight

8306               	; 	' ======================================================================
8307               	; 	' OBSTACLES
8308               	; 	' ======================================================================
8309               	; upd_obst:
8310               cvb_UPD_OBST
8311               	; 	' ONE ACCUMULATOR PER KIND, NOT PER SLOT. Every hazard of a kind moves at
8312               	; 	' that kind's speed, so three calls a pass serve all eight slots.
8313               	; 	'
8314               	; 	' Per-slot would have meant up to eight calls of O(fdv) work every pass --
8315               	; 	' the positive feedback loop CLAUDE.md 3A warns about, where a slow pass is
8316               	; 	' made slower by the very code meant to take the loop rate out of the
8317               	; 	' game's behaviour. It also saves the eight bytes an obacc() would cost,
8318               	; 	' and ColecoVision RAM is the tighter budget.
8319               	; 	pacc = oacb
8320 D480 D820  54 	movb @cvb_OACB,@cvb_PACC
     D482 20C8     
     D484 2122     
8321               	; 	psp64 = obsp
8322 D486 D820  54 	movb @cvb_OBSP,@cvb_PSP64
     D488 213F     
     D48A 212A     
8323               	; 	GOSUB pace_step
8324 D48C 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     D48E A980     
8325 D490 D6BA     	data cvb_PACE_STEP
8326               	; 	oacb = pacc
8327 D492 D820  54 	movb @cvb_PACC,@cvb_OACB
     D494 2122     
     D496 20C8     
8328               	; 	ospb = pspd
8329 D498 D820  54 	movb @cvb_PSPD,@cvb_OSPB
     D49A 2146     
     D49C 20F1     
8330               	; 	pacc = oacc
8331 D49E D820  54 	movb @cvb_OACC,@cvb_PACC
     D4A0 20C9     
     D4A2 2122     
8332               	; 	psp64 = ocsp

     D4A6 216D     
     D4A8 212A

     D4AC A980

8352               	; 	' THE BOUNCE PHASE, ON THE SAME CLOCK AS THE JUMP AND AT ITS OLD PERIOD.
8353               	; 	'
8354               	; 	' The jump has always advanced by the frame delta (`kjf = kjf + fdv`) while
8355               	; 	' the bounce advanced once per PASS -- so whether a jump cleared a ball
8356               	; 	' depended on how busy the screen was, and the frame-by-frame property
8357               	; 	' checkball.py proves was exact only at fdv = 1.
8358               	; 	'
8359               	; 	' Stepping the phase by fdv directly would fix the clock and wreck the
8360               	; 	' game: a 32-phase arc would drop from about 1.35 s to 0.55 s, balls
8361               	; 	' bouncing 2.4x faster everywhere. So it gets a fraction of its own --
8362               	; 	' 25/64 of a phase per frame is 23.4 phases a second, against the 23.8 it
8363               	; 	' runs at today. Same bounce, now the same clock as the thing that has to

8580 D6B6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8581 D6B8 0450  20 	b *r0
8582               	;
8583               	; 	' ONE STEP OF A FRACTIONAL SPEED, FOR WHOEVER IS MOVING.
8584               	; 	'
8585               	; 	' `pacc` in and out, `psp64` in (sixty-fourths of a pixel per FRAME),
8586               	; 	' `pspd` out (whole pixels this pass). CVBasic has no locals, so the caller
8587               	; 	' stages its own accumulator in and takes it back out; that costs four
8588               	; 	' statements against the fourteen this used to be, written out per actor.
8589               	; 	'
8590               	; 	' Harry had the only copy. Kelly, the hazards and the bounce phase all need
8591               	; 	' the same thing -- everything the round clock judges has to advance by
8592               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAMES, or the loop rate becomes a difficulty dial and where the
8593               	; 	' player stands decides the outcome (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
8594               	; 	'
8595               	; 	' TWO DRAINS CAP THE STEP AT 2 PER FRAME whatever psp64 says (CLAUDE.md
8596               	; 	' 3A: N drains cap the speed at N, and the surplus leaks into a byte that
8597               	; 	' wraps). Every caller is under that: Kelly 102/64 = 1.6, the fastest
8598               	; 	' hazard 76/64 = 1.2, Harry 57/64 = 0.9, the bounce phase 25/64 = 0.4.
8599               	; 	'
8600               	; 	' FIVE FRAMES, AND THREE WAS TOO FEW. At 2 px a frame five frames is 10 px,
8601               	; 	' and to skip a position window an actor must start outside it on one side
8602               	; 	' and land outside on the other, which needs 12. Three was the original bug
8603               	; 	' in miniature: the lift screen runs at almost exactly 20 passes a second,
8604               	; 	' which IS fdv = 3, so every hitch there lost a frame. The clamp exists for
8605               	; 	' a genuine stall and at five it only fires for one.
8606               	; pace_step:

9092               	;
9093               	; 	' THE SIXTEENTH-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen
9094               	; 	' (2), so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here
9095               	; 	' and is spent as whole pixels. THREE IFs, not a loop and not a divide:
9096               	; 	' `%` and `/` both compile to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this
9097               	; 	' runs every pass.
9098               	; 	'
9099               	; 	' THE NUMBER OF DRAINS IS A CEILING ON THE SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
9100               	; 	' POINT OF THIS COMMENT. Each IF can spend one whole pixel, so N drains
9101               	; 	' cap hspd at N px per pass NO MATTER WHAT hsp64 SAYS. The invariant is:
9102               	; 	'
9103               	; 	'     hacc is under 16 on entry, and hsp64 is at most 48,
9104               	; 	'     so hacc is under 64 here and three drains always suffice.
9105               	; 	'
9106               	; 	' KEEP hsp64 <= 48. This was quarter-pixels with TWO drains and the same
9107               	; 	' invariant written the same way -- "hsp4 is at most 8, so it can never
9108               	; 	' need a third" -- and it was correct until somebody set hsp4 = 9 without
9109               	; 	' re-reading it. The crook then ran at a flat 2.0 px/pass while every
9110               	; 	' comment, the design table and assets/checkchase.py all said 2.25, and
9111               	; 	' the surplus quarter leaked into hacc and wrapped the byte every 256
9112               	; 	' passes. Nothing failed; he simply arrived two thirds of a screen short
9113               	; 	' of his own escape. checkchase.py now counts these IFs and refuses a
9114               	; 	' speed the drains cannot deliver.
9115               	; 	'
9116               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
9117               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
9118               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
9119               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
9120               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
9121               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
9122               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
9123               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
9124               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
9125               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
9126               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
9127               	; 	' flight looking busy.
9128               	; 	'
9129               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
9130               	; 	'
9131               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
9132               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
9133               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
9134               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
9135               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
9136               	; 	'
9137               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
9138               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
9139               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
9140               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
9141               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN

9222               cv585
9223               	;
9224               	; 	' HE WALKS BY REAL TIME, NOT BY LOOP PASS -- one accumulate-and-drain per
9225               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAME rather than per pass. Everything that moves here used to be
9226               	; 	' per pass while the CLOCK is paced by the frame delta, so a busy screen
9227               	; 	' slowed Harry in real seconds and the clock did not slow with him: the
9228               	; 	' same uninterrupted escape finished with nine timer units in hand from a
9229               	; 	' light screen and none at all from the lift screen, decided by where the
9230               	; 	' player happened to be standing. Now he covers the same ground per second
9231               	; 	' wherever anybody is (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
9232               	; 	'
9233               	; 	' A LOOP, NOT A MULTIPLY. `*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, and reading a
9234               	; 	' 16-bit variable straight after one returns the product's HIGH word
9235               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A). fdv is 1..3 here, so this is at most three adds.
9236               	; 	'
9237               	; 	' SIXTY-FOURTHS, AND ONLY TWO DRAINS -- both fall out of accumulating per
9238               	; 	' FRAME instead of per pass. He covers under a pixel a frame, so two drains
9239               	; 	' are ample where the per-pass version needed three; and the finer unit is
9240               	; 	' what makes the speed tunable at all. In sixteenths one step was about six
9241               	; 	' SECONDS of his route, so nothing landed near the buzzer; in sixty-fourths
9242               	; 	' it is about one and a half. Keep hsp64 <= 128: hacc is under 64 on entry,
9243               	; 	' so 64 + 128 stays inside a byte and two drains always suffice.
9244               	; 	'
9245               	; 	' AND THE STEP IS CLAMPED TO THREE FRAMES' WORTH, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL.
9246               	; 	' Two tests sample his position once a pass and neither interpolates:
9247               	; 	'
9248               	; 	'   ARRIVAL   `hdx < 6` around the escalator or the roof door -- an ELEVEN
9249               	; 	'             pixel window. A step over 10 px can jump clean across it and
9250               	; 	'             he never arrives at all.
9251               	; 	'   THE CATCH `hdd < CATCHR` is 12, a 23-wide band, and Kelly closes 4 of
9252               	; 	'             it himself -- so Harry over ~18 px could pass through the Kop
9253               	; 	'             between two passes without either sample being inside it.
9254               	; 	'
9255               	; 	' The clamp and the drains live in pace_step now, with the reasoning; the
9256               	; 	' numbers above are what sized them. Measured: TIME 02 on the lift screen
9257               	; 	' against TIME 04 on a light one when the clamp was 3, and 00 against 09
9258               	; 	' before any of this.
9259               	; 	'
9260               	; 	' The ESCALATOR RIDE is deliberately untouched: it returns above this,
9261               	; 	' clocked one step per pass to stay locked to the moving staircase. A rider
9262               	; 	' advanced by the frame delta either drifts off the treads or makes the
9263               	; 	' steps run backwards (CLAUDE.md 3A, DESIGN.md 0f). It is about 3% of his
9264               	; 	' journey.
9265               	; 	'
9266               	; 	' `hanim` still advances by hspd and is still an ODOMETER -- pixels
9267               	; 	' travelled, not time -- so the run cycle stays locked to the ground at any
9268               	; 	' rate. A 9 px step moves it exactly one pose of its 8 px beat.
9269               	; 	pacc = hacc

9700               	;
9701               	; 	' ======================================================================
9702               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9703               	; 	' ======================================================================
9704               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9705               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9706               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9707               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9708               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9709               	; 	'
9710               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9711               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9712               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9713               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9714               	; 	' broken.
9715               	; 	'
9716               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9717               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9718               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9719               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9720               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9721               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9722               	; 	'
9723               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9724               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9725               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9726               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9727               	; coll_obst:
9728               cvb_COLL_OBST
9729               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the

     DE88 0300

     E100 2098     
10026               	; 	' THE FLOOR CLEARS WHEN YOU ARE HIT, and stays clear until you re-enter
10027               	; 	' the screen. Nine seconds is a heavy penalty on a fifty-unit clock, and
10028               	; 	' taking it while still standing among the things that charged it -- with
10029               	; 	' a second hazard 48 px behind the first -- is how one mistake becomes
10030               	; 	' three. Clearing the row makes the penalty a single event you can walk
10031               	; 	' away from.
10032               	; 	'
10033               	; 	' BOTH SLOTS, WHATEVER IS IN THEM. Radios used to be exempt: they are
10034               	; 	' CHARACTERS stamped into the name table rather than sprites, so zeroing
10035               	; 	' the kind would have stopped them colliding while leaving them plainly
10036               	; 	' visible on the shelf, and a fixture that is still there has to still be
10037               	; 	' there. That was a reason to UNDRAW them, not a reason to keep them --
10038               	; 	' and it left the one hazard that cannot leave on its own as the only one
10039               	; 	' the mercy did not cover. haz_off now takes the characters with it.
10040               	; 	'
10041               	; 	' NOTHING RESTORES IT HERE. load_band repopulates the band from the
10042               	; 	' template, and it runs only on a seam crossing or a round start, so
10043               	; 	' "until the screen is re-entered" costs no state and no timer: leave and
10044               	; 	' come back and the hazards are simply placed again.
10045               	; 	GOSUB haz_gone
10046 E102 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     E104 A980     
10047 E106 D2FC     	data cvb_HAZ_GONE
10048               	; 	' A HIT DOES NOT END A JUMP. This used to force ST_RUN and zero the arc,
10049               	; 	' which dropped him straight down out of mid-air onto whatever he happened
10050               	; 	' to be over -- and since the arc is ballistic and ignores the stick once
10051               	; 	' he is airborne (0l), that read as the game taking the controls away
10052               	; 	' rather than as a hit landing. The penalty is the nine units and the
10053               	; 	' knock flash; where he comes down is still his own arc.
10054               	; 	'
10055               	; 	' A ducking Kop is still stood up, because the crouch is a held pose and
10056               	; 	' there is nothing to interrupt.
10057               	; 	IF klst <> ST_JUMP THEN
10058 E108 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     E10A 21B0

     E10E 0100

10215               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
10216               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
10217               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
10218               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
10219               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
10220               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
10221               	; 	'

10288               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
10289               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
10290               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
10291               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
10292               	; 	'
10293               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
10294               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
10295               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
10296               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
10297               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
10298               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
10299               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

10444               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
10445               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
10446               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
10447               	; 		'
10448               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
10449               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
10450               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
10451               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
10452               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
10453               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1
10454 E418 0200  20 	li r0,14336   ; 56*256
     E41A 3800     
10455 E41C D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_KQ
     E41E 21DA     
10456               	; 		' BITS 3 AND 4, NOT 2 AND 3 -- A POSE EVERY 8 PIXELS, which is
10457               	; 		' Harry's beat (hanim AND 8 / AND 16) and now means the same thing,
10458               	; 		' because both counters hold pixels travelled. On the old 4-count
10459               	; 		' beat an odometer would have flipped his legs every single pass at
10460               	; 		' 4 px a pass, which is a blur rather than a run. checkanim reads
10461               	; 		' these bits out of the source, and fails if two bands of one figure
10462               	; 		' run on different ones -- so the body below moves with them.
10463               	; 		IF kanim AND 8 THEN kq = kq + 4

10741               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10742               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10743               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10744               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10745               	; 	'
10746               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10747               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10748               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10749               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10750               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10751               	; 	'
10752               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10753               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10754               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10755               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10756               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
10757               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
10758               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.
10759               	; 	hp = P_HBODY

10841               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
10842               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10843               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10844               	; 	'
10845               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10846               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10847               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10848               	; 	' a jump.
10849               	; 	'
10850               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
10851               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
10852               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
10853               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
10854               	; 	'
10855               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
10856               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
10857               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
10858               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
10859               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
10860               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
10861               	; 	'
10862               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
10863               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
10864               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
10865               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
10866               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
10867               	; 	'
10868               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
10869               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
10870               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
10871               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
10872               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.
10873               	; 	IF hanim AND 8 THEN hy = hy - 1

11340 EB02 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11341 EB04 0450  20 	b *r0
11342               	;
11343               	; 	' ======================================================================
11344               	; 	' THE SCANNER
11345               	; 	' ======================================================================
11346               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
11347               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
11348               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
11349               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
11350               	; 	'
11351               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
11352               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
11353               	; 	'
11354               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
11355               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
11356               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
11357               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
11358               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
11359               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
11360               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
11361               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
11362               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
11363               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
11364               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
11365               	; esc_tick:
11366               cvb_ESC_TICK
11367               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
11368               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
11369               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
11370               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11371               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
11372 EB06 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EB08 219E     
11373 EB0A 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     EB0C 0100     
11374 EB0E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11375 EB10 1402  14 	jhe cv800
11376 EB12 0460  28 	b @cv799
     EB14 EB2A     
11377               cv800
11378               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
11379 EB16 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EB18 219E     
11380 EB1A 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     EB1C 0700     
11381 EB1E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11382 EB20 1A02  14 	jl cv802
11383 EB22 0460  28 	b @cv801
     EB24 EB2A     
11384               cv802
11385 EB26 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11386 EB28 0450  20 	b *r0
11387               cv801
11388               	; 	END IF
11389               cv799
11390               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
11391               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
11392               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
11393               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
11394               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
11395               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
11396               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
11397               	; 	'
11398               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
11399               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
11400               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
11401               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
11402               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
11403               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
11404               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
11405               	; 	escp = escp + 1
11406 EB2A D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EB2C 20B1     
11407 EB2E 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     EB30 0100     
11408 EB32 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EB34 20B1     
11409               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
11410 EB36 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     EB38 0400     
11411 EB3A 9040  18 	cb r0,r1

11413 EB3E 0460  28 	b @cv803
     EB40 EB48

11415 EB42 04C0  14 	clr r0

     EB46 20B1     
11417               cv803
11418               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
11419               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
11420               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11421               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11422               	; 	' 7 an east one.
11423               	; 	'
11424               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
11425               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
11426               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
11427               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
11428               	; 	'
11429               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
11430               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
11431               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
11432               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
11433               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
11434               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
11435               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
11436               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
11437               	; 	'
11438               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
11439               	; 	' move at all.
11440               	; 	IF klsc = 0 THEN

11699 ED84 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11700 ED86 0450  20 	b *r0
11701               	;
11702               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11703               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11704               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11705               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11706               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11707               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11708               	; 	'
11709               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11710               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11711               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11712               	; 	'
11713               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11714               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11715               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11716               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11717               	; 	'
11718               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11719               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11720               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11721               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11722               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11723               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11724               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11725               	; 	'
11726               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11727               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11728               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11729               	; 	'
11730               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11731               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11732               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11733               	; scan_furn:
11734               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11777               	; 		' for the same three floors. Every input is a pure function of fl --
11778               	; 		' scan_base, the side lookup, the masks, scan_pat -- and scan_or1 is an
11779               	; 		' OR, so doing it twice cannot differ from doing it once. Sixteen
11780               	; 		' statements, about 320 bytes, and nine VDP writes a floor that nothing
11781               	; 		' on screen could reflect.

11784               	; 		' original is left behind -- scan_escs is this block plus the colour
11785               	; 		' write that was added later, and it carried a copy of the explanatory
11786               	; 		' comment as well as the code.

12074               	;
12075               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
12076               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
12077               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
12078               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
12079               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
12080               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12081               	; scan_wipe:
12082               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
12083               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
12084               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
12085               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
12086               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
12087               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
12088               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
12089               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
12090               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
12091               	; 	'
12092               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
12093               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
12094               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
12095               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
12096               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
12097               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
12098               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
12099               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
12100               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
12101               	; 	'
12102               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
12103               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
12104               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
12105               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
12106               	; 	'
12107               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
12108               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
12109               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
12110 F01E 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     F020 1680

     F024 20E4

12177               	;
12178               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12179               	; 	'
12180               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12181               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12182               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12183               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12184               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12185               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12186               	; 	'
12187               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12188               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12189               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12190               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12191               	; 	'
12192               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12193               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12194               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12195               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12196               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12197               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12198               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12199               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12200               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12201               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12202               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
12203               	; 	say = 3 - elvl

     F628 AA32     
12891               	;
12892               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
12893               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
12894               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
12895               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
12896               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
12897               	; 	'
12898               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
12899               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
12900               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
12901               	; 	'
12902               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
12903               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
12904               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
12905               	; 	'
12906               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
12907               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12908               	; bonus_count:
12909               cvb_BONUS_COUNT
12910               	; 	#bval = 100
