     F710 F662     
13011               	;
13012               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
13013               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
13014               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
13015               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
13016               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
13017               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
13018               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
13019               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
13020               	; 	'
13021               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
13022               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
13023               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13024               	;
13025               	; do_escape:

13065               	; 	' THE MESSAGE BOX, ON THE GAME SCREEN AND NOT INSTEAD OF IT.
13066               	; 	'
13067               	; 	' It used to CLS before GAME OVER, so the last thing the player saw was
13068               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
13069               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store
13070               	; 	' stays; the message sits in the middle of it.
13071               	; 	'
13072               	; 	' THE BOX IS THE FONT'S OWN BACKGROUND. Every font character is black on
13073               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
13074               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and
13075               	; 	' no new characters, and it reads against the store because nothing else on

13080               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it

13252               	;
13253               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13254               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13255               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13256               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13257               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13258               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13259               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13260               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13261               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
13262               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
13263               	; 	' Harry was caught.

13362               	;
13363               	; 	' ======================================================================
13364               	; 	' SOUND
13365               	; 	' ======================================================================
13366               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
13367               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
13368               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
13369               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
13370               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
13371               	; sfx_tick:
13372               cvb_SFX_TICK

13717 FCC0 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
13718 FCC2 0450  20 	b *r0
13719               	;
13720               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
13721               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
13722               	; 	'
13723               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
13724               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
13725               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
13726               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
13727               	; 	'
13728               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
13729               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
13730               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
13731               	; 	'
13732               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
13733               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
13734               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
13735               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
13736               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
13737               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
13738               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
13739               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
13740               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
13741               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
13742               	; 	#if TI994A
13743               	; 	BANK 1
13744      033A     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

0008 FCCB   FF     	byte 255
0009 FCCC FF       	byte 255
0010 FCCD   FF     	byte 255

0012 FCCF   FF     	byte 255
0013 FCD0 FF       	byte 255

13751               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
13752               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
13753               	; 	'
13754               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
13755               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
13756               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
13757               	; 	'
13758               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
13759               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
13760               	; 	' letter after it.
13761               	;
13762               	; font_bits:
13763               cvb_FONT_BITS

13882               	;
13883               	; 	' THE COLOUR TABLE, 472 bytes of the same value ($14 = black ink on dark blue).
13884               	; 	'
13885               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE COLOR 32,59,font_col, which is ONE synchronous call that
13886               	; 	' writes all three screen thirds (define_color always does the LDIRVM3 triple
13887               	; 	' copy). It replaced a VPOKE loop that wrote this one value 1,416 times paced by
13888               	; 	' 24 WAITs -- the largest scripted delay in the boot, and the reason the title
13889               	; 	' screen used to fill in visibly rather than appearing at once.
13890               	; 	'
13891               	; 	' EIGHT BYTES PER CHARACTER, not one. Supply fewer and DEFINE COLOR reads
13892               	; 	' whatever follows in ROM as colour data, with no error at build or run time.
13893               	;
13894               	; font_col:
13895               cvb_FONT_COL
13896               	; 	DATA BYTE $14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14	' space
13897 6202 1414     	byte >14,>14,>14,>14,>14,>14,>14,>14

14014               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
14015               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14016               	; 	'
14017               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
14018               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
14019               	; 	' being small sprites.
14020               	; 	'
14021               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
14022               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
14023               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
14024               	; 	'
14025               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
14026               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
14027               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
14028               	;
14029               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
14030               cvb_SPR_KELLY
14031               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00

15026               	;
15027               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
15028               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
15029               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
15030               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
15031               	;
15032               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
15033               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
15034               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
15035               	; 	' unreadable.
15036               	;
15037               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

15149               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
15150               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
15151               	; 	'
15152               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
15153               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
15154               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
15155               	; 	'
15156               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
15157               	; 	' blit is
15158               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
15159               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
15160               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
15161               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
15162               	; 	'
15163               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
15164               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
15165               	; 	'
15166               	; 	' template ids:
15167               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
15168               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
15169               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
15170               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
15171               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
15172               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF0
15173               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF1
15174               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF2
15175               	; 	'   8 T_ROOF3
15176               	; 	'   9 T_ROOF4
15177               	; 	'   10 T_ROOF5
15178               	; 	'   11 T_ROOF6
15179               	; 	'   12 T_ROOF7
15180               	; 	'   13 T_END_W
15181               	; 	'   14 T_END_E
15182               	;
15183               	; stor_tpl:	' 15 templates x 160 bytes
15184               cvb_STOR_TPL
